.
As a direct consequence of
x\equiv_{\mathcal{A}}y
, making the above reasoning with
y
instead of
x
results dealing with identical intermediate sets of states and sets of stacks.
Hence, we proved that all configurations of the from
(t,v,\theta)
where
t\in Q
and
\theta\in\varPhi_{u}(q_{I},\bot)
reachable from
(q_{I},uu_{0}xv_{0}v,\bot)
is also reachable from
(q_{I},uu_{0}yv_{0}v,\bot)
. ∎

Lemma
29

\proofsubparagraph

*Statement.
For every
L\subseteq\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}
, if
\equiv_{L}
has finite index then
L
is a
\widehat{\Sigma}
-OPL.

Proof.

Consider a language
L\subseteq\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}
such that
\equiv_{L}
has finitely many equivalence classes.
We construct a deterministic OPA that recognizes
L
and whose states are based on the equivalence classes of
{\equiv_{L}}
.
Given
w\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}
, we denote
[w]
its equivalence class with respect to
{\equiv_{L}}
.
We construct
\mathcal{A}=(Q,\{q_{0}\},F,\Delta)
with the set of states
Q=\{([u],[v])\mid u,v\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}\}
, the initial state
q_{0}=([\varepsilon],[\varepsilon])
, the set of accepting states
F=\{([\varepsilon],[w])\mid w\in L\}
, and the
\widehat{\Sigma}
-driven transition function
\Delta\colon Q\times\Sigma\times(\Gamma^{+}\cup\{\bot\})\rightarrow Q\times(\Sigma\cup\{\varepsilon\})\times(\Gamma^{+}\cup\{\bot\})
, where
\Gamma=\Sigma\times Q
, is defined as follows:

\Delta
maps
(([u],[v]),a,\langle b,([u^{\prime}],[v^{\prime}])\rangle\theta)
to
(([a],[\varepsilon]),\varepsilon,\langle a,([u],[v])\rangle\langle b,([u^{\prime}],[v^{\prime}])\rangle\theta)
if
b\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}a
,
it returns
(([uva],[\varepsilon]),\varepsilon,\langle a,([u^{\prime}],[v^{\prime}])\rangle\theta)
if
b\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}a
,
and
(([u^{\prime}],[v^{\prime}uv]),a,\theta)
if
b\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}a
.

Invariants.

We show that the automaton
\mathcal{A}
satisfies the following invariants after reaching the state
([u],[v])\in Q
from the initial state
q_{0}
:

1.

The top of the stack is
u^{\triangleright}
if
u\neq\varepsilon
, and
\bot
otherwise.

2.

All outgoing on
a\in\Sigma
satisfies
va\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}
and
v\neq\varepsilon\Rightarrow v^{\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}a
.

3.

u\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}}
.

4.

u^{\triangleright}v\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}}
and
v\neq\varepsilon\Rightarrow u^{\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}v^{\triangleleft}
.

We prove all invariants together by induction on the length of the run from
q_{0}
that reaches the state
([u],[v])\in Q
.
The run starts in
q_{0}=([\varepsilon],[\varepsilon])
which trivially satisfies all invariants.
By induction we assume the invariants to hold for all run of length
n>0
.
Let
([u],[v])
be the state reached after
n
transitions,
a\in\Sigma
be the incoming letter and
(b,([u^{\prime}],[v^{\prime}]))
be the top of the stack.

If
b\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}a
, the automaton
\mathcal{A}
performs a push-transition to reach
([a],[\varepsilon])
, which satisfies (1) by definition of push-transition.
The invariants (2, 3, 4) hold trivially.

If
b\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}a
, the automaton
\mathcal{A}
performs a shift-transition to reach
([uva],[\varepsilon])
, which satisfies (1) by definition of the shift-transition.
Since a shift-transition is triggered, the stack is not
\bot
.
By the induction hypothesis, (1) ensures that
u^{\triangleright}=b
and (2) ensures that
va\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}
.
Consequently and because
b\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}a
, we have that
uva\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}}
, i.e., (3) is preserved.
The invariants (2, 4) hold trivially.

If
b\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}a
, the automaton
\mathcal{A}
performs a pop-transition to reach
([u^{\prime}],[v^{\prime}uv])
.
Since a pop-transition is triggered, the stack is not
\bot
.
By the induction hypothesis, (1) ensures that
u^{\triangleright}=b
, thus
u^{\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}a
.
In particular
u\neq\varepsilon
, and thus
u^{\triangleleft}\in\Sigma
.
Additionally, the induction hypothesis gives us that
va\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}
and
v\neq\varepsilon\Rightarrow v^{\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}a
by (2).
We have that
uva\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}
because
u^{\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}a
,
u\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}}
and
{{}^{u}[v]^{a}}
from (2, 3, 4).
Finally, (4) ensures that
u^{\triangleright}v\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}}
.

Since
([u^{\prime}],[v^{\prime}])
is on the stack, there exists a strict prefix of the current run that ends in
([u^{\prime}],[v^{\prime}])
and such that popping
(b,([u^{\prime}],[v^{\prime}]))
recovers its stack.
By the induction hypothesis on such smaller run, (1) ensures that if
u^{\prime}=\varepsilon
then the stack is
\bot
otherwise the top is
u^{\prime\triangleright}
, and (3) ensures that
u^{\prime}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}}
.
So, (1) and (3) are directly preserved.
Continuing the induction hypothesis,
(2) ensures that
v^{\prime}u^{\triangleleft}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}
and (4) ensures that
u^{\prime\triangleright}v^{\prime}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}}
and
v^{\prime}\neq\varepsilon\Rightarrow u^{\prime\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}v^{\prime\triangleleft}
.
In the case where
u^{\prime}\neq\varepsilon
and
v^{\prime}=\varepsilon
, we get that
v^{\prime}uv\neq\varepsilon\Rightarrow u^{\prime\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}(v^{\prime}uv)^{\triangleleft}
since
u^{\prime\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}u^{\triangleleft}
as the automaton
\mathcal{A}
pushed
([u^{\prime}],[v^{\prime}])
on the stack while reading
u^{\triangleleft}
.
The other cases are immediate.
Moreover,
u^{\prime\triangleright}v^{\prime}uv\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}}
comes as we established that
u^{\prime\triangleright}v^{\prime}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}}
,
u\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}}
,
u^{\triangleright}v\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}}
, and
u^{\prime\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}u^{\triangleleft}
.
Hence (4) is preserved.
For the invariant (2), we established that
uva\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}
and
v^{\prime}u^{\triangleleft}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}
, which implies that
v^{\prime}uva\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}
.
We also established that
v\neq\varepsilon\Rightarrow v^{\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}a
and
u^{\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}a
, which implies that
(v^{\prime}uv)^{\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}a
.
In particular, the invariant (2) is preserved.

Determinism.

For all states
([a],[\varepsilon]),([b],[\varepsilon])\in Q
reachable in
\mathcal{A}
with a push-transition,
if
a\neq b
then
a\not\approx b
which implies that
a\not\equiv_{L}b
.
Reciprocally, if
a\equiv_{L}b
then
a\approx b
, which implies that
a=b
.

For all states
([u_{1}],[v_{1}]),([u_{2}],[v_{2}])\in Q
and all
a\in\Sigma
, let
([u_{1}v_{1}a],[\varepsilon]),([u_{2}v_{2}a],[\varepsilon])
be two states reachable in
\mathcal{A}
with a shift-transition.
We show that, if
u_{1}\equiv_{L}u_{2}
and
v_{1}\equiv_{L}v_{2}
then
u_{1}v_{2}a\equiv_{L}u_{2}v_{2}a
.
If
v_{1}=\varepsilon
, then
v_{2}\equiv_{L}v_{1}
implies
v_{2}=\varepsilon
.
Also
u_{1}\equiv_{L}u_{2}
implies
u_{1}a\equiv_{L}u_{2}a
by chain-monotonicity of
{\equiv_{L}}
.
Otherwise, if
v_{1}\neq\varepsilon
, then
v_{2}\equiv_{L}v_{1}
implies
v_{2}\neq\varepsilon
.
Furthermore,
{{}^{u_{1}}[v_{1}]^{a}}
and
{{}^{u_{1}}[v_{2}]^{a}}
, since
u_{1}\approx u_{2}
and the invariants (2) and (4) hold.
In particular,
u_{1}^{\triangleright}v_{2}a\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}}
.
By chain-monotonicity of
{\equiv_{L}}
, we have
v_{1}\equiv_{L}v_{2}
implies
u_{1}v_{1}a\equiv_{L}u_{1}v_{2}a
, and
u_{1}\equiv_{L}u_{2}
implies
u_{1}v_{2}a\equiv_{L}u_{2}v_{2}a

Hence,
u_{1}v_{1}a\equiv_{L}u_{2}v_{2}a
, by transitivity of
{\equiv_{L}}
.

For all states
([u_{1}],[v_{1}])
,
([u_{2}],[v_{2}])
,
([u^{\prime}_{1}],[v^{\prime}_{1}])
,
([u^{\prime}_{2}],[v^{\prime}_{2}])\in Q
, we let
([u^{\prime}_{1}],[v^{\prime}_{1}u_{1}v_{1}])
and
([u^{\prime}_{2}],[v^{\prime}_{2}u_{2}v_{2}])
be two states reachable in
\mathcal{A}
with a pop-transition.
We show that, if
u_{1}\equiv_{L}u_{2}
,
v_{1}\equiv_{L}v_{2}
, and
v^{\prime}_{1}\equiv_{L}v^{\prime}_{2}
, then
v^{\prime}_{1}u_{1}v_{1}\equiv_{L}v^{\prime}_{2}u_{2}v_{2}
.
As a direct consequence of the invariants, we have that
{{}^{v^{\prime}_{1}u_{1}}[u_{1}]^{\varepsilon}}
,
{{}^{v^{\prime}_{1}u_{1}}[u_{2}]^{\varepsilon}}
,
{{}^{\varepsilon}[v^{\prime}_{1}u_{1}v_{2}]^{\varepsilon}}
,
{{}^{\varepsilon}[v^{\prime}_{1}u_{2}v_{2}]^{\varepsilon}}
,
{{}^{\varepsilon}[v^{\prime}_{1}]^{u_{2}v_{2}}}
, and
{{}^{\varepsilon}[v^{\prime}_{2}]^{u_{2}v_{2}}}
.
Additionally,
v^{\prime}_{1}u^{\triangleleft},v^{\prime}_{1}u_{2}^{\triangleleft}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}
and
u_{1}^{\triangleright}v_{2},u_{2}^{\triangleright}v_{2}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}}
.
By chain-monotonicity of
{\equiv_{L}}
, we have
v_{1}\equiv_{L}v_{2}
implies
v^{\prime}_{1}u_{1}v_{1}\equiv_{L}v^{\prime}_{1}u_{1}v_{2}
,
{\equiv_{L}}
,
u_{1}\equiv_{L}u_{2}
implies
v^{\prime}_{1}u_{1}v_{2}\equiv_{L}v^{\prime}_{1}u_{2}v_{2}
, and
v^{\prime}_{1}\equiv_{L}v^{\prime}_{2}
implies
v^{\prime}_{1}u_{2}v_{2}\equiv_{L}v^{\prime}_{2}u_{2}v_{2}
.
Hence,
v^{\prime}_{1}u_{1}v_{1}\equiv_{L}v^{\prime}_{2}u_{2}v_{2}
, by transitivity of
{\equiv_{L}}
.

Correctness.

We have
L(\mathcal{A})=L
since
[w]\cap L=\varnothing
or
[w]\subseteq L
, for all
w\in\Sigma^{*}
.
More precisely, every
w\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}
admits a unique run
(([\varepsilon],[\varepsilon]),w,\bot)\mathrel{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\hbox to11.78pt{\vbox to2.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.3381pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

since
\mathcal{A}
is deterministic.
By induction we prove that, for all
x,y\in\Sigma^{*}
, all
\theta\in\Gamma^{+}\cup\{\bot\}
, if
(q_{0},xy,\bot)\mathrel{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\hbox to11.78pt{\vbox to2.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.3381pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}^{*}(([u_{0}],[v_{0}],y,\theta)
then there exist
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
(a_{i},([u_{i}],[v_{i}]))_{i\in\{1\dots n\}}
such that
\theta=\langle a_{1},([u_{1}],[v_{1}])\rangle...\langle a_{n},([u_{n}],[v_{n}])\rangle\bot
and
x\equiv_{L}u_{n}v_{n}\dots u_{0}v_{0}
.
In particular, we get that
w\equiv_{L}w^{\prime}
implying that
w\in L(\mathcal{A})
iff
([\varepsilon],[w^{\prime}])\in F
iff
w^{\prime}\in L
iff
w\in L
.
The base case, when the run has length
n=0
, is trivial since
x=\varepsilon
and
\theta=\bot
.
Suppose that the property holds for all runs of length
n
, we prove that it holds for runs of length
n+1
.
Let
z=u_{n}v_{n}\dots u_{0}v_{0}
.
In the case where the last transition is a push-transition that reads
a\in\widehat{\Sigma}
from
([u_{0}],[v_{0}])
.
If
z=\varepsilon
then
x=\varepsilon
since
x\equiv_{L}z
.
Hence
xa\equiv_{L}za
holds trivially.
Otherwise
z\neq\varepsilon
.
Since a push-transition is triggered and
u_{0}^{\triangleright}v_{0}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}}
by invariant (2), we have that
{{}^{\varepsilon}[z]^{a}}
.
Since
x\equiv_{L}z
and
{{}^{\varepsilon}[x]^{a}}
holds as well then we get
xa\equiv_{L}za
by chain-monotonicity.
In the case where the last transition is a pop-transition from
([u_{0}],[v_{0}])
.
Then the reached state is
([u_{1}],[v_{1}u_{0}v_{0}])
and the property is trivially preserved.
In the case where the last transition is a shift-transition that reads
a\in\widehat{\Sigma}
from
([u_{0}],[v_{0}])
.
Since a shift-transition is triggered, we that the
u_{0}^{\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}a
by invariant (1).
In particular
u_{0}\neq\varepsilon
.
By invariant (2), if
v_{0}\neq\varepsilon
then
v_{0}^{\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}a
.
Due to
x\equiv_{L}z
, we have that
x^{\triangleright}=v_{0}^{\triangleright}
.
So,
xa\equiv_{L}za
since
{{}^{\varepsilon}[x]^{a}}
and
{{}^{\varepsilon}[z]^{a}}
.
Otherwise if
v_{0}=\varepsilon
.
Due to
x\equiv_{L}z
and
u_{0}\neq\varepsilon
, we have that
x^{\triangleright}=u_{0}^{\triangleright}
.
So,
xa\equiv_{L}za
since
{{}^{\varepsilon}[x]^{a}}
and
{{}^{\varepsilon}[z]^{a}}
.
∎

Proposition
35

\proofsubparagraph

*Statement.
For every OPA
\mathcal{A}
,
\leqslant_{\mathcal{A}}
is a computable chain-monotonic well quasi-order.

Proof.

Let
\widehat{\Sigma}
be an operator precedence alphabet, and
\mathcal{A}=(Q,I,F,\Delta)
be an OPA.
We only show that the structural quasi-order
\leqslant_{\mathcal{A}}
is chain-monotonic since the rest is argued before the statement in the main body of the paper.
Let us define the following relation over
\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}
:

x\ll y\iff\forall q\in Q,\forall\gamma\in\Gamma\cup\{\bot\}\bigwedge\begin{cases}f_{x}(q,\gamma)\subseteq f_{y}(q,\gamma)\\
g_{x}(q,q^{\prime},\gamma)\subseteq g_{y}(q,q^{\prime},\gamma)\\
(\varPhi_{x}(q,\gamma))^{\top}\subseteq(\varPhi_{y}(q,\gamma))^{\top}\end{cases}

Recall that for every
x,y\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}
we have
x\leqslant_{\mathcal{A}}y
iff
x\approx y
and
x\ll y
, where
{\approx}
is the chain equivalence.
In particular, we want to show that for every
x,y,u,v,u_{0},v_{0}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}
such that
u_{0}x^{\triangleleft},u_{0}y^{\triangleleft}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}
,
x^{\triangleright}v_{0},y^{\triangleright}v_{0}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}}
,
{{}^{u}[u_{0}xv_{0}]^{v}}
,
{{}^{u}[u_{0}yv_{0}]^{v}}
, and
x\leqslant_{\mathcal{A}}y
, we have
uu_{0}xv_{0}v\leqslant_{\mathcal{A}}uu_{0}yv_{0}v
.
Since the chain equivalence
{\approx}
is chain-monotonic, we only need to show that
{\ll}
is chain-monotonic, i.e., we have
uu_{0}xv_{0}v\ll uu_{0}yv_{0}v
for every
x,y,u,v,u_{0},v_{0}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}
as above.

Let
\star\notin\Sigma
be a fresh letter for which we extend the precedence relation with
a\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}\star
for all
a\in\Sigma
.
Let
w\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}
,
q,q^{\prime}\in Q
, and
\gamma\in\Gamma\cup\{\bot\}
.
Recall the following:

f_{w}(q,\gamma)=\{q_{w}\in Q\mid\exists\gamma_{w}\in\Gamma^{+}\cup\{\bot\},(q,w\star,\gamma)\mathrel{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\hbox to11.78pt{\vbox to2.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.3381pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

\varPhi_{w}(q,\gamma)=\{\gamma_{w}\in\Gamma^{+}\cup\{\bot\}\mid\exists q_{w}\in Q,(q,w\star,\gamma)\mathrel{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\hbox to11.78pt{\vbox to2.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.3381pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

Now, let
x,y,u,v,u_{0},v_{0}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}
such that
u_{0}x^{\triangleleft},u_{0}y^{\triangleleft}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}
,
x^{\triangleright}v_{0},y^{\triangleright}v_{0}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}}
,
{{}^{u}[u_{0}xv_{0}]^{v}}
,
{{}^{u}[u_{0}yv_{0}]^{v}}
, and
x\leqslant_{\mathcal{A}}y
.
Since
x\leqslant_{\mathcal{A}}y
implies
x\approx y
, which implies
x^{\triangleleft}=y^{\triangleleft}
and
x^{\triangleright}=y^{\triangleright}
, it is clear that from the definitions that
f_{uu_{0}xv_{0}v}(q,\gamma)\subseteq f_{uu_{0}yv_{0}v}(q,\gamma)
and
\varPhi_{uu_{0}xv_{0}v}(q,\gamma)\subseteq\varPhi_{uu_{0}yv_{0}v}(q,\gamma)

for all
q\in Q
and
\gamma\in\Gamma\cup\{\bot\}
.
Intuitively, the reasoning for this is as follows:

\mathcal{A}
reaches a set of configurations after processing
uu_{0}
.
For every configuration in this set, consider the state and the top stack symbol given as inputs to
f_{x}
and
f_{y}
, as well as
\varPhi_{x}
and
\varPhi_{y}
, for whose outputs we know the inclusion relation above.
It means that after reading
uu_{0}x
and
uu_{0}y
from any state and top stack symbol we have the same relations as well.
Now, consider this time the states and top stack symbols of the configurations reached after
uu_{0}x
and
uu_{0}y
.
Proceeding the computation from these configurations with the suffix
v_{0}v
clearly preserves the inclusion relation for the sets of states reached.
For the sets of stacks, note that
{{}^{u}[u_{0}xv_{0}]^{v}}
and
{{}^{u}[u_{0}yv_{0}]^{v}}
, therefore the suffix
v_{0}v
pops the stack beyond what has been pushed while reading
x
and
y
, which is the same for both words.
Finally, for all
q,q^{\prime}\in Q
and all
\gamma\in\Gamma\cup\{\bot\}
, we have that
g_{uu_{0}xv_{0}v}(q,q^{\prime},\gamma)\subseteq g_{uu_{0}yv_{0}v}(q,q^{\prime},\gamma)
from
\varPhi_{uu_{0}xv_{0}v}(q,\gamma)\subseteq\varPhi_{uu_{0}yv_{0}v}(q,\gamma)
which implies that
\{(q^{\prime},\varepsilon,\gamma_{w})\mid\gamma_{w}\in\varPhi_{uu_{0}xv_{0}v}(q,\gamma)\}\subseteq\{(q^{\prime},\varepsilon,\gamma_{w})\mid\gamma_{w}\in\varPhi_{uu_{0}yv_{0}v}(q,\gamma)\}
.
Therefore,
uu_{0}xv_{0}v\ll uu_{0}yv_{0}v
, and thus
uu_{0}xv_{0}v\leqslant_{\mathcal{A}}uu_{0}yv_{0}v
, implying that
\leqslant_{\mathcal{A}}
is chain-monotonic. ∎

Lemma
39

\proofsubparagraph

*Statement.
Let
\mathcal{A}=(Q,I,F,\Delta)
be an OPA, and let
\Gamma=\Sigma\times Q
.
Considering
\vec{U}^{a,b,c}_{s,t}=\varepsilon
when
a=b
and
s=t
, otherwise
\vec{U}^{a,b,c}_{s,t}=\varnothing
.
The following holds for all
n>0
:

\texttt{Cat}^{n}(\vec{U}^{a,b,c}_{s,t}){=}\big\{u\mid(s,uc,\alpha){\mathrel{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\hbox to11.78pt{\vbox to2.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.3381pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

where, for all
a\in\widehat{\Sigma}
, the set of stack symbols
\varTheta_{a}\subseteq\Gamma\cup\{\bot\}
is defined by
\varTheta_{a}=\{\bot\}
if
a=\varepsilon
, and
\varTheta_{a}=\{\langle a,q\rangle\mid q\in Q\}
otherwise.

Proof.

For readability, we define the set of runs
\varOmega^{a,b,c}_{s,t,n}(w)
, for all
a,b,c\in\widehat{\Sigma}
,
s,t\in Q
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
w\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}
as follows.

\varOmega^{a,b,c}_{s,t,m}(w)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c|c}\rho&\bigwedge\begin{array}[]{l}\rho=(s,wc,\alpha)\mathrel{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\hbox to11.78pt{\vbox to2.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.3381pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\alpha\in\varTheta_{a}\land\beta\in\varTheta_{b}\\
aw\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}}\land wc\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}\land(aw)^{\triangleright}=b\end{array}\end{array}\right\}

We reformulate the statement as
u\in\texttt{Cat}^{n}(\vec{U}^{a,b,c}_{s,t})
if and only if
\varOmega^{a,b,c}_{s,t,2n}(u)\neq\varnothing
.
For all
u\in\texttt{Cat}^{n}(\vec{U}^{a,b,c}_{s,t})
, it takes a simple induction on
|u|
to prove that
\varOmega^{a,b,c}_{s,t,2n}(u)\neq\varnothing
, because
Cat
follows runs of
\mathcal{A}
and preserves the invariants

m\leq 2|u|
,

au\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}}
,

uc\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}
,

(au)^{\triangleright}=b
by definition.
Next, we prove that
\varOmega^{a,b,c}_{s,t,2n}(u)\neq\varnothing
implies
u\in\texttt{Cat}^{n}(\vec{U}^{a,b,c}_{s,t})
, where
n=|u|
.

The proof goes by induction on the structure of
w=auc
.
In the base case,
w
does not admit any subchains, i.e.,
\lambda(w)=w
.
If
\varOmega^{a,b,c}_{s,t,2n}(u)\neq\varnothing
holds, then,
au\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}}
and
uc\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}
.
Together with
\lambda(w)=w
, it implies that
u\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}}
.
Hence, any run witnessing
\varOmega^{a,b,c}_{s,t,2n}(u)\neq\varnothing
performs exclusively shift-transitions.
Actually, since that run performs exclusively shift-transitions, it length must be
n=|u|
.
The base case goes by induction on such a witnessing run of the length
n
.
Having
n=0
implies
u=\varepsilon
and
(s,ua,\alpha)=(t,a,\beta)
.
In fact
\vec{U}
is defined such that
\varepsilon\in\vec{U}^{a,b,c}_{s,t,a}
exactly when
a=b
and
s=t
.
By induction, we assume that there exists a run of
\varOmega^{a,b,c}_{s,t,2n}(u)
of length
n=|u|
of the form
(s,uc,\alpha)\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.4pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\hbox to12.85pt{\vbox to12.81pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.73502pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope

where

and

.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}^{n-1}(t,c,\beta)
witnesses
\varOmega^{a^{\prime},b,c}_{q,t,2|v|}(v)\neq\varnothing
.
So,
v\in\texttt{Cat}^{|v|}(\vec{U}^{a^{\prime},b,c}_{q,t})
by induction hypothesis.
Finally,
u\in\texttt{Cat}^{n}(\vec{U}^{a,b,c}_{s,t})
by definition of
CatShift
.

In the inductive step, we assume that
w
is of the form
a_{0}u_{0}a_{1}u_{1}\ldots a_{k}u_{k}a_{k+1}
such that for all
0\leq i\leq k
, either
{{}^{a_{i}}[u_{i}]^{a_{i+1}}}
or
u_{i}=\varepsilon
, and
\lambda(a_{0}a_{1}\dots a_{k+1})=a_{0}a_{1}\dots a_{k+1}
.
It is worth emphasizing that
a_{0}=a
, and
b=(au)^{\triangleright}
and
a_{k+1}=c
by definition of
w
.
Also
k>0
, since otherwise
\lambda(w)=w
, which is the base case of this induction.
As in the base case, if
\varOmega^{a,b,c}_{s,t,2n}(u)\neq\varnothing
then
au\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}}
and
uc\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}}}}{\makebox[6.3778pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\geq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}}}}}
, which implies
a_{0}a_{1}\dots a_{k+1}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}}
since
\lambda(a_{0}a_{1}\dots a_{k+1})=a_{0}a_{1}\dots a_{k+1}
.
If there exists a run
\rho
witnessing
\varOmega^{a_{0},(au)^{\triangleright},a_{k+1}}_{s,t,2n}(u)\neq\varnothing
with
n=|u|
then, due to

a_{0}a_{1}\dots a_{k+1}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}}
, for all
1\leq i\leq k
, there exists a run
\rho_{i}
over
u_{i}
such that
\rho=\rho_{0}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.4pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\hbox to13.95pt{\vbox to11.08pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.28627pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 12.98 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{9.38113pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.77 0 L -1.66 2.21 L 0 0 L -1.66 -2.21}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{2.04674pt}{4.533pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\rho_{1}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.4pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\hbox to13.95pt{\vbox to11.08pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.28627pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 12.98 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{9.38113pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.77 0 L -1.66 2.21 L 0 0 L -1.66 -2.21}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{2.04674pt}{4.533pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\dots\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.4pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\hbox to14.21pt{\vbox to11.08pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.41388pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 12.98 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{9.38113pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.77 0 L -1.66 2.21 L 0 0 L -1.66 -2.21}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{1.91913pt}{4.533pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\rho_{k}
.
Since
{{}^{a_{i}}[u_{i}]^{a_{i+1}}}
for all
0\leq i\leq k
, each
\rho_{i}
witnesses
\varOmega^{a_{i},(a_{i}u_{i})^{\triangleright},a_{i+1}}_{s_{i},t_{i},2n_{i}}(u_{i})\neq\varnothing
with
n_{i}=|u_{i}|
and
s_{i},t_{i}\in Q
.
However, the induction hypothesis cannot be apply on
\varOmega^{a_{i},(a_{i}u_{i})^{\triangleright},a_{i+1}}_{s_{i},t_{i},2n_{i}}(u_{i})\neq\varnothing
, as it may have the same chain structure as
w
.
Hence, we proceed by cases to prove that
u_{i}\in\texttt{Cat}^{n_{i}}(\vec{U}^{a_{i},(a_{i}u_{i})^{\triangleright},a_{i+1}}_{s_{i},t_{i}})
.
In the case when
u_{i}=\varepsilon
the run
\rho_{i}
witnessing
\varOmega^{a_{i},(a_{i}u_{i})^{\triangleright},a_{i+1}}_{s_{i},t_{i},2n_{i}}(u_{i})\neq\varnothing
, or equivalently
\varOmega^{a_{i},a_{i},a_{i+1}}_{s_{i},t_{i},2n_{i}}(u_{i})\neq\varnothing
must be empty since
a_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}a_{i+1}
.
This implies that
s_{i}=t_{i}
.
In fact, we trivially have that
\varepsilon\in\vec{U}^{a_{i},a_{i},a_{i+1}}_{s_{i},s_{i},2n_{i}}(u_{i})
by definition of
\vec{U}
.
Equivalently,
u_{i}\in\texttt{Cat}^{n_{i}}\vec{U}^{a_{i},(a_{i}u_{i})^{\triangleright},a_{i+1}}_{s_{i},t_{i},2n_{i}}(u_{i})\neq\varnothing
.
Otherwise
u_{i}\neq\varepsilon
.
Let
b^{\prime}_{i}=u_{i}^{\triangleleft}
,
b_{i}=u_{i}^{\triangleright}
, and
u^{\prime}_{i}
be such that
u_{i}=b^{\prime}_{i}u^{\prime}_{i}
.
In this cases, the run
\rho_{i}
witnessing
\varOmega^{a_{i},(a_{i}u_{i})^{\triangleright},a_{i+1}}_{s_{i},t_{i},2n_{i}}(u_{i})\neq\varnothing
must starts with a push-transition on
b^{\prime}_{i}
since
a_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}b^{\prime}_{i}
, and must ends with a pop-transition on
b_{i}
since
b_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\gtrdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\gtrdot$}}}a_{i+1}
.
In other words, there exists some run
\rho^{\prime}_{i}
witnessing
\varOmega^{b^{\prime}_{i},b_{i},a_{i+1}}_{s^{\prime}_{i},t^{\prime}_{i},2n^{\prime}_{i}}(u^{\prime}_{i})\neq\varnothing
where
|u^{\prime}_{i}|=n^{\prime}_{i}
and
s^{\prime}_{i},t^{\prime}_{i}\in Q
.
Now, we can apply the induction hypothesis on
w^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{i}u^{\prime}_{i}a_{i+1}
which is a strict subchain of
w
.
Hence,
u^{\prime}_{i}\in\texttt{Cat}^{n^{\prime}_{i}}(\vec{U}^{b^{\prime}_{i},b_{i},a_{i+1}}_{s^{\prime}_{i},t^{\prime}_{i}})
.
The rest of the proof is straightforward.
For all
1\leq i\leq k
, we get
u_{i}\in\texttt{Cat}^{n_{i}}\vec{U}^{a_{i},(a_{i}u_{i})^{\triangleright},a_{i+1}}_{s_{i},t_{i},2n_{i}}(u_{i})\neq\varnothing
by definition of
CatChain
and since
{{}^{a_{i}}[u_{i}]^{a_{i+1}}}
.
Finally, we can prove that
u\in\texttt{Cat}^{n}(\vec{U}^{a,b,c}_{s,t})
by induction on
k
, by definition of
CatShift
and since
a_{0}a_{1}\dots a_{k+1}\in\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}}
.
∎

Lemma
41

\proofsubparagraph

*Statement.
Let
\preccurlyeq
be a chain-monotonic quasi-order over
\widehat{\Sigma}^{*}
.
For every
A=(Q,I,F,\Delta)
and
\vec{X},\vec{Y}
such that
\mathfrak{B}(\vec{X}^{a,b,c}_{s,t}\preccurlyeq\vec{Y}^{a,b,c}_{s,t})
holds for all
s,t\in Q
and all
a,b,c\in\Sigma\cup\{\varepsilon\}
, we have
\mathfrak{B}(\texttt{Cat}(\vec{X}^{a,b,c}_{s,t})\preccurlyeq\texttt{Cat}(\vec{Y}^{a,b,c}_{s,t}))
holds also for all
s,t\in Q
and all
a,b,c\in\Sigma\cup\{\varepsilon\}
.

Proof.

Assume that
\mathfrak{B}(\vec{X}^{a,b,c}_{s,t}\preccurlyeq\vec{Y}^{a,b,c}_{s,t})
holds for all
s,t\in Q
and all
a,b,c\in\Sigma\cup\{\varepsilon\}
.
In particular, for all
y_{0}\in\vec{Y}^{a,b,c}_{s,t}
, there exists
x_{0}\in\vec{X}^{a,b,c}_{s,t}
such that
x_{0}\preccurlyeq y_{0}
.
Consider
y\in\texttt{Cat}(\vec{Y}^{a,b,c}_{s,t})
, we show that there exists
x\in\texttt{Cat}(\vec{X}^{a,b,c}_{s,t})
such that
x\preccurlyeq y
.
By definition of
Cat
, there are three cases: Either (1)
y\in\vec{Y}^{a,b,c}_{s,t}
or, (2)
y\in\texttt{CatShift}(\vec{Y}^{a,b,c}_{s,t})
, or (3)
y\in\texttt{CatChain}(\vec{Y}^{a,b,c}_{s,t})
.
We show (2) since (3) can be prove similarly and (1) is trivial from
\mathfrak{B}(\vec{X}^{a,b,c}_{s,t}\preccurlyeq\vec{Y}^{a,b,c}_{s,t})
.
Suppose that
y
is of the form
y_{1}b^{\prime}y_{2}
for some
y_{1}\in\vec{Y}^{a,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}_{s,s^{\prime}}
,
b^{\prime}\in\Sigma
, and
y_{2}\in\vec{Y}^{b^{\prime},b,c}_{t^{\prime},t}
.
By hypothesis, there exist
x_{1}\in\vec{X}^{a,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}_{s,s^{\prime}}
and
x_{2}\in\vec{X}^{b^{\prime},b,c}_{t^{\prime},t}
such that
x_{1}\preccurlyeq y_{1}
and
x_{2}\preccurlyeq y_{2}
.
If
y_{1}=\varepsilon
then
x_{1}=\varepsilon
and thus
x_{1}b^{\prime}\preccurlyeq y_{1}b^{\prime}
.
If
y_{1}\neq\varepsilon
then
y_{1}^{\triangleright}=a^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.00003pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[8.00003pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[8.00003pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[8.00003pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}b^{\prime}
and
x_{1}^{\triangleright}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.00003pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[8.00003pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[8.00003pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\scriptsize$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}}}}{\makebox[8.00003pt]{{\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny$\leq$}}{\makebox[0.0pt]{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}}}}b^{\prime}
since
y_{1}\approx x_{1}
.
We have that
{{}^{\varepsilon}[x_{1}b^{\prime}]^{\varepsilon}}
and
{{}^{\varepsilon}[y_{1}b^{\prime}]^{\varepsilon}}
.
So,
x_{1}b^{\prime}\preccurlyeq y_{1}b^{\prime}
.
We have that
{{}^{\varepsilon}[x_{1}b^{\prime}y_{2}]^{\varepsilon}}
and
{{}^{\varepsilon}[y_{1}b^{\prime}y_{2}]^{\varepsilon}}
.
So,
x_{1}b^{\prime}y_{2}\preccurlyeq y_{1}b^{\prime}y_{2}
.
If
b^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}y_{2}
then
b^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\lessdot$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\lessdot$}}}x_{2}
.
We have that
{{}^{x_{1}b^{\prime}}[x_{2}]^{\varepsilon}}
and
{{}^{x_{1}b^{\prime}}[y_{2}]^{\varepsilon}}
.
So,
x_{1}b^{\prime}x_{2}\preccurlyeq x_{1}b^{\prime}y_{2}
.
By transitivity,
x_{1}b^{\prime}x_{1}\preccurlyeq x_{1}b^{\prime}y_{2}
.
If
b^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}y_{2}
then
b^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.8pt}{\scriptsize$\dot{=}$}}{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\tiny$\dot{=}$}}}x_{2}
.
We have that
{{}^{\varepsilon}[x_{1}b^{\prime}x_{2}]^{\varepsilon}}
and
{{}^{\varepsilon}[x_{1}b^{\prime}y_{2}]^{\varepsilon}}
.
So,
x_{1}b^{\prime}x_{2}\preccurlyeq x_{1}b^{\prime}y_{2}
.
By transitivity,
x_{1}b^{\prime}x_{1}\preccurlyeq y_{1}b^{\prime}y_{2}
.
∎

Theorem
42

\proofsubparagraph

*Statement.
The algorithm from Figure
7
terminates and decides language inclusion.

Proof.

First, we show that the inclusion algorithm from Figure
7
always terminates.
From the definition of
Cat
and the constant
\vec{U}
, we have that each component of
\vec{X}
holds a finite set of words after executing finitely many instructions.
The halting conditions of the repeat/until loop is effectively computable.
Indeed, deciding
\mathfrak{B}(X\preccurlyeq Y)
where
X
,
Y
are finite sets and
\preccurlyeq
a decidable quasi-order, can be done by checking whether
X\subseteq Y
and that for every
y\in Y
there exists
x\in X
such that
x\preccurlyeq y
.
Additionally, the quasi-order
\preccurlyeq
is a well-quasiorders and thus, there is no infinite sequence
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
{{}_{\preccurlyeq}}{\upharpoonleft}X_{1}\subsetneq{{}_{\preccurlyeq}}{\upharpoonleft}X_{2}\subsetneq\dots
.
Since
\mathfrak{B}(X\preccurlyeq Y)
is defined by
X\subseteq Y\land{{}_{\preccurlyeq}}{\upharpoonleft}X={{}_{\preccurlyeq}}{\upharpoonleft}Y
and since
Cat
only extends the upward closures of the components of
\vec{X}
, we find that the repeat/until loop must terminate after finitely many iterations.

Now, we show that the inclusion algorithm from Figure
7
is correct.
Supposing that the algorithm returns
ko
.
Once
\vec{X}
reached the fixpoint computed by the repeat/until loop, all
w\in\vec{X}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon,\varepsilon}_{q_{I},q_{F}}
where
q_{I}\in I
and
q_{F}\in F
belong to
L_{1}
by Lemma
39
.
Hence, when the algorithm returns
ko
, then
L_{1}\not\subseteq L_{2}
.
Conversely, supposing that
L_{1}\not\subseteq L_{2}
, in particular let
w\in L_{2}\setminus L_{1}
.
In fact,
w
belongs to
\texttt{Cat}^{n}(\vec{U}_{q_{I},q_{F}}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon,\varepsilon})
for some
q_{I}\in I
,
q_{F}\in F
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, by Lemma
39
.
Additionally, observe that once
\vec{X}
reached the fixpoint computed by the repeat/until loop,
\vec{X}_{q_{I},q_{F}}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon,\varepsilon}
is a base of
\texttt{Cat}^{n}(\vec{U}_{q_{I},q_{F}}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon,\varepsilon})
, i.e.
\mathfrak{B}(\vec{X}_{q_{I},q_{F}}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon,\varepsilon}\preccurlyeq\texttt{Cat}^{n}(\vec{U}_{q_{I},q_{F}}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon,\varepsilon}))
.
This can be proved by induction thanks to Lemma
41
.
Hence, there exists
w_{0}\in\vec{X}_{q_{I},q_{F}}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon,\varepsilon}
such that
w_{0}\preccurlyeq w
.
Since
\preccurlyeq
satisfies that
(w_{0}\preccurlyeq w\land w_{0}\in L_{2})\implies w\in L_{2}
, we get
w_{0}\notin L_{2}
and thus the algorithm returns
ko
.
∎
